Maya is fixed: she no longer gets the video, so she can't repeat details you never taught her. Before, one AI call decided who spoke and wrote the question, so it had to see the transcript even on Maya's turns. Now the code picks the speaker first, and only Omar's turns include the transcript and key concepts.

**Real test:** I ran 12 AI turns using a made-up video full of details the explanation never mentioned, such as thylakoid, Rubisco, night breathing and cave plants.
- **Maya:** she spoke 7 times, and everything she said came from the explanation. For example: "how can thin air turn into something solid like a plant?"
- **Omar:** he still used the video, asking for "the exact part of the leaf where the water gets split".
- **Follow-ups:** vague answers still got a follow-up from whoever asked. The one case I tested for a skipped question moved on in the unit tests, not in a live call.

**Other changes:**
- Every question was opening with "Wait,", so I banned that opener. The next run stopped doing it.
- Your answers now get a separate quick AI check to decide on a follow-up, so most questions take two Gemini calls instead of one.

**Things to know:**
- **Rate limit:** my testing hit a Gemini 429 once, and the extra call makes that more likely in a fast class. I didn't check what the app shows you when it happens.
- **Repeated questions:** Maya asks much the same thing again after Omar's turn, because her question isn't really tied to what just happened. All 3 of her test questions that hit this were about air turning into something solid.
- **Not tested:** I ran this directly against Gemini, not through the app screen, so the class flow in the UI still needs a run with this change. The test explanation and transcript were also short.

The tests, typecheck, lint and the other local checks pass. The production build still has to run before the PR.

Raised hands and "End class" both work in the app now, matching Arif's wireframe. I tested them in the browser with your signed-in session, which also covered the app-screen check the Maya fix was missing.

**How the class works now:**
- **Hands up:** both students raise a hand, and the screen shows "Hands up: pick who to answer" and "Question 1 of 4".
- **Picking:** tapping a student shows their question, and the other student's question stays hidden until you pick them.
- **After an answer:** the other student keeps their hand up, and only the student you answered thinks of a new question, so it costs no extra AI questions overall. Their first questions are the exception: both are written at the start, so that's two calls.
- **End class:** it finishes straight away and drops any questions you didn't pick, and I tested this with and without answering first.
- **Maya:** a question nobody has picked yet isn't shown to the other student, so Omar's video details can't reach her that way. She'll only hear them if you answer him.
- **Events:** I added `class_hand_picked`, and `class_finished` now records whether you ended early.

**One rule removed:** I dropped the limit of one follow-up in a row. Follow-ups used to be limited so a student couldn't keep pressing you, but now you can pick someone else or end the class.

**Problems still there:**
- **Reaction before any answer:** Maya's first question can open with "That got pretty mixed up in my head", as if she's reacting to something when you haven't answered yet.
- **Slow questions:** each one still takes 10–12 seconds, even though the AI part is 1–2 seconds. My earlier guess about the database is still untested, but the slowness is confirmed on this version.
- **AI failure after an answer:** if Gemini fails at that point, your answer is saved but that student gets no new hand. With no other hand up, the class would look finished, or stay stuck on your answer until you restart. This isn't handled yet.

**Not tested:**
- A full class to question 4; I stopped after one answer.
- The follow-up question itself. The vague-answer check ran, but my tap on Omar's second question missed, so I never saw what he asked.

**Test data:** my two test classes on the photosynthesis video are saved in your account, and Maya and Omar may bring them up in later classes as past conversations.

Yes, a few more. Most of these come from reading the code, and I only saw the second one happen in the app. They're in the order I'd fix them.

**Bugs**
1. **Unheard questions can leak into later classes:** if you press back with hands still up, those questions stay saved. The next class treats them as past conversation, marked "(no answer)". That means Omar's video details can reach Maya, and the students can mention questions you never heard. "End class" clears them, but leaving the screen doesn't.
2. **Leaving loses the class:** the screen doesn't resume, so pressing back or reloading sends you to a blank explanation. The old class stays unfinished.
3. **Memory isn't tied to the video:** students get your last 6 questions from any earlier class, so a question about photosynthesis could turn up in a class on a different video. That can feel like real memory or like a mistake.
4. **Answers can appear out of order:** the conversation is sorted by when each question was created, not when you answered it. If you answer Omar first and then an older Maya question, Maya's shows above his. The AI reads the class in that same wrong order.

**Things that feel wrong**

5. **False reactions:** "That got pretty mixed up in my head" and "That explanation actually makes sense" appear on opening questions. The second is praise, which your rules ban, and neither is a reaction to an answer.
6. **Undeserved praise:** "Class finished. Nice teaching." shows even if you end the class without answering anything.
7. **Very short explanations work:** typing "hi" starts a full class.
8. **Omar isn't aimed at what you missed:** he picks from all the video's concepts, because scoring (8X-15) doesn't exist yet. Asking about something you left out is partly luck for now.

**Gaps a judge will notice**

9. **The class is a dead end:** it finishes with a sentence and no gaps, score or next step.
10. **You can't get back to a video:** Home has no list of past videos, so you have to paste the link again.
11. **It still looks like the template:** the title says "MyApp", and the Explore and Activity tabs are still there.
12. **The student cards are bare:** Arif's wireframe shows "Beginner" or "Critical" on each card, and ours show only the name.

I'd fix 1, 4, 5, 6 and 7 before opening this PR, because they're small and sit right inside this change. Then 2 and 3 can follow as the next piece of work, while 8 and 9 wait on scoring.

| 2 | Leaving loses the class | Reopening a video brings back the unfinished class, with the same hands up | Reloaded mid-class in the app |

| 5 | False reactions | With no answer to react to, students just ask; they do react after a real answer | Seen in the app, e.g. "That clears up how the sap changes…" |
| 6 | Undeserved praise | The end message now says "Class finished. You answered 2 questions." or "Class ended. You didn't answer any questions." | Saw the first message in the app; the second wasn't tested |

The AI's follow-up question also now takes part in the reaction rule. I only saw follow-ups run in the logs, not a weak-answer follow-up on screen.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=14 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:28:42.790Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:28
chars: 1049

Because on your Mac it's a program that's already installed, and Vercel only ships your app's code. The API calls `yt-dlp`, or `uvx yt-dlp`, as an outside command. Vercel runs each request in a small temporary Node.js environment, which normally has neither of those. `yt-dlp` isn't an npm package either, so `npm install` won't bring it along.

That part is fixable: we could include the standalone `yt-dlp` program in the deploy so the code calls it directly.

The bigger risk is YouTube itself. It often blocks or challenges requests from cloud servers like Vercel's, even when your home connection works fine. So even with `yt-dlp` included, caption requests could fail, and we already saw 429 rate limits while testing from your own machine. When that happens, the app falls back to Gemini, which is slower and limited to 30-minute videos.

I haven't tried any of this on Vercel, so this is what I expect, not something I've seen fail. Testing it takes one deployed video read, and that's the first thing I'd do before relying on a public link.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=15 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:31:20.531Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:31
chars: 2259

Captions through `yt-dlp` won't work on Vercel, and both of my concerns were right. I ran a throwaway function on Vercel's servers (US East) three times on your photosynthesis video, and all three failed the same way:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `yt-dlp` already there | No |
| `uvx` or Python already there | No |
| Downloading the standalone `yt-dlp` | Worked, and it ran |
| Reading the video on YouTube | **Blocked**: "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" |

So including `yt-dlp` in the deploy wouldn't help, because YouTube refuses Vercel's servers.

**What this means for a hosted app:**
- **Video reading should still work, through Gemini only:** the code already falls back when `yt-dlp` fails, and Gemini fetches the video itself, so YouTube's block on Vercel shouldn't matter. I haven't tested that on Vercel.
- **Reading will be slower:** captions take seconds, while Gemini took 14 seconds for a short video.
- **Long videos have no length check:** without `yt-dlp`, the app doesn't know how long a video is, so the 30-minute limit never applies. A long video would go to Gemini anyway, costing more and possibly hitting Vercel's time limit.
- **Some public videos may fail:** the fallback gets the title from a YouTube lookup that also refuses videos with embedding turned off, so those would show "We can't open this video." That lookup also comes from Vercel's servers, so I can't promise YouTube allows it.

**Ways around it:**
1. **Use Gemini only when hosted:** the simplest option, and enough for a hackathon. Add a way to get the video's length, such as the YouTube Data API, so the limit still works.
2. **Pass YouTube cookies to `yt-dlp`:** this is what the error suggests, but it ties the app to a personal Google account, can get that account flagged, and breaks when the cookies expire. I wouldn't do it.
3. **Run reading on a small separate server or proxy:** the most reliable for captions, but it's more to build and pay for.

I'd go with option 1 if you want a public link. For Monday's feedback, nothing changes, because your local setup still uses captions.

The throwaway project is deleted from your Vercel account and no longer appears in your project list. It held no secrets, and none of your repo's code was deployed.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=16 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:33:19.051Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:33
chars: 4069

Yes, there are real options besides Gemini, and I'd use a hosted transcript API, starting with Supadata. The block is on the server's IP address, not on `yt-dlp` itself, so switching libraries usually doesn't help. The fix is to get the captions through an address YouTube doesn't block.

| Option | What it is | Cost | Catch |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hosted transcript API**, like [Supadata](https://supadata.ai/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api) or [TranscriptAPI](https://transcriptapi.com/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api-compared) | Your server makes one normal HTTPS call, and they deal with YouTube | Supadata lists about 100 free credits a month; TranscriptAPI reportedly starts around $5 per 1,000 requests | Depends on another company; prices vary between sources |
| **[youtube-transcript-api](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api) with rotating residential proxies** | Open-source Python library; its maintainer recommends Webshare's residential proxies | Library is free, proxies are paid | Python, so it doesn't fit our Node API directly; [one user was still blocked on AWS](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api/issues/593), and free-tier proxies don't work |
| **`yt-dlp` with [bgutil PO tokens](https://pypi.org/project/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider/)** | Plugin that makes requests look more legitimate | Free, plus a server to run it | Its own docs say datacenter addresses usually still need cookies, so this is the risky account-tied route again |
| **Fetch from a home or office machine** | [Run only the transcript step there](https://dev.to/jeffreyinman/fixing-sign-in-to-confirm-youre-not-a-bot-for-youtube-transcripts-on-a-server-3i1b) and send results to the cloud | Free | A machine has to stay on; useless for a real product |
| **YouTube's official Data API** | The official route | Free quota | Reportedly needs OAuth to download captions, usually from the video's owner, so it likely won't work for any public video. I didn't verify this |

**Why Supadata:**
- **Smallest change:** it's one HTTP call added before the Gemini fallback, with no proxies or Python.
- **No-caption videos:** comparison articles say it has an AI fallback for these. I'd keep our Gemini fallback anyway.
- **Free tier:** at about one credit per transcript, it should be enough for the hackathon demo.
- **Video length:** our limit check needs the duration, so I'd have to confirm the API returns it.

**Before relying on these:**
- Most of the comparison pages come from the vendors themselves, so treat their rankings, speed and reliability claims with caution, and check prices on their own pricing pages.
- These services and proxies work around YouTube's blocks, which is a grey area under YouTube's terms. That's fine for a demo, but worth rechecking if this becomes a real product.

If you go ahead, you'd need to sign up for a free key and add it to the env file yourself, without pasting it in chat. I'd then run the same test from Vercel, because a vendor's claim isn't proof it works there.

Sources:
- [Guide to cloud IP blocks](https://github.com/hxckya/youtube-transcript-ip-blocked-guide)
- [What fixes RequestBlocked in 2026](https://dev.to/lmslay/youtube-transcript-api-requestblocked-on-your-server-what-actually-works-in-2026-1pak)
- [Fixing the "not a bot" error on a server](https://dev.to/jeffreyinman/fixing-sign-in-to-confirm-youre-not-a-bot-for-youtube-transcripts-on-a-server-3i1b)
- [Vercel and Lambda blocks issue](https://github.com/hallelx2/youtube-transcript-ts/issues/1)
- [youtube-transcript-api](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api)
- [Webshare still blocked report](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api/issues/593)
- [Supadata comparison](https://supadata.ai/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api)
- [TranscriptAPI comparison](https://transcriptapi.com/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api-compared)
- [bgutil PO token provider](https://pypi.org/project/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider/)
- [Data API suggestion in TwoRiversReporter](https://github.com/AndreRobitaille/TwoRiversReporter/issues/87)

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=17 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]

Supadata is built, but I can't test it against the real service until you add an API key. It's uncommitted on a new branch, `devvshayan/supadata-captions`, cut from the latest `main`, which already includes your merge of PR #10. The tests pass with my two new ones, but they only cover turning Supadata's captions into our transcript format, not a live call.

**How a video gets read now:**
1. **`yt-dlp` goes first:** it's free, and your local setup should keep working the same way.
2. **Supadata is next:** it only runs if `yt-dlp` is missing, blocked or rate-limited, which on Vercel should be every time. It fetches the title, the length and any existing captions.
3. **Gemini is the fallback:** it handles videos with no captions.

With a Supadata key, hosted reads get the video's length back, so the length limits should work again.

**To add your key:**
1. Sign up at [supadata.ai](https://supadata.ai) and create a key.
2. Add this line to `apps/web/.env.local`, and don't paste the key in chat:

```bash
SUPADATA_API_KEY=your_key_here
```

3. Add the same variable in Vercel when you deploy.

Once it's in, I'll restart the API and read a video with `yt-dlp` hidden, so it has to go through Supadata. I'll also check how many credits a read costs, because the docs I read didn't say.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=18 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T08:33:25.737Z
time: 2026-10-31 08:33
chars: 2101

Supadata works with your real key. With `yt-dlp` and `uvx` hidden to imitate Vercel, reading the photosynthesis video took about 5 seconds and returned all 412 words in 30 segments. That's the same word and segment count as the transcript stored for that video on hosted, and Gemini took about 8 seconds on this test.

**Testing found two problems, both fixed:**
- **429 errors:** I was sending both calls at once, so the transcript was refused and the read silently fell back to Gemini. The calls now run one after another, with one short retry.
- **Rough timestamps:** Supadata's default chunks run about 25 seconds, so concept timestamps could be far off. I now ask for smaller chunks, which brings segments down to roughly 10 seconds and timestamps in line with the other read paths.

**Cost:** each read uses 2 credits, one for the transcript and one for the title and length. The free 100 credits a month cover about 50 video reads, assuming your 100 is the full allowance. Testing used about 12 of them. Once a video is read it's saved, so reading it again doesn't use credits. On your local machine it only spends credits when `yt-dlp` fails or is rate-limited.

**Limits to know about:**
- **Not tested on Vercel:** I imitated it locally. Supadata does the YouTube fetching, so it shouldn't matter where the call comes from, but the first real deploy still needs checking. Add `SUPADATA_API_KEY` in Vercel at that point.
- **Quota running out:** if the 100 credits run out, a read should quietly fall back to Gemini, but I haven't seen that happen.
- **Videos with no speech:** on Big Buck Bunny, which has no dialogue, it returned just "Thank you." as the whole transcript. A nearly speechless video could therefore be treated as having captions, and I left that as is.
- **Videos with no captions:** I didn't test one with speech, so Gemini taking over in that case is unconfirmed.

Tests, typecheck, lint and formatting pass on the final code. The other local checks passed just before the last small fix, and the production build still has to run before the PR, as it stops the local API.

> When the chlorophyll in the leaves receives sunlight, the process begins by transforming the raw sap into elaborated sap… it leaves out the specific chemical or cellular details…

**How it works:**
- **Private:** only you see it. It isn't saved, and neither student receives it.
- **From the video only:** it explains the question in two or three short sentences using only the transcript, and says what the video leaves out.
- **Real timestamps:** the server takes the time from the transcript, so the AI can't invent one.
- **Speed and cost:** it took under 4 seconds, and each question is only fetched once, so reopening the panel costs nothing.
- **Tracking:** answering after opening it saves the turn as `needed_help`, which I confirmed on hosted. That's the outcome the assessment screen will show, and opening the panel fires a new `class_help_opened` event.

The order is the same on your Mac and on a server:
1. **`yt-dlp`:** on your Mac it normally gets the captions for free, and Supadata isn't called unless it fails or is rate-limited.
2. **Supadata:** on Vercel, `yt-dlp` isn't installed, so that step should fail right away and move here.
3. **Gemini:** handles videos with no captions, or anything Supadata can't get.

So keeping `yt-dlp` shouldn't slow hosted reads or cost anything there. It just saves Supadata credits while you work locally.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=27 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T09:40:11.950Z
time: 2026-10-31 09:40
chars: 2531

In production, a person uses the phone app, which calls your Next.js API on Vercel, backed by hosted Supabase and Gemini. It isn't deployed yet, so this is how it's built to run, and I've only tested parts of it on Vercel.

**1. Sign in**
The app signs in through Supabase and sends a token with each request, and the server checks that token.

**2. Paste a YouTube link**
- If that person already read this video, the saved copy is used, with no credits or AI cost.
- Otherwise the API tries `yt-dlp`, which isn't installed on Vercel, so that step fails right away.
- **Supadata** then gets the title, length and existing captions, for 2 credits.
- If the video has no captions, or Supadata fails, **Gemini** watches the video and writes the transcript. That's slower and costs AI tokens.
- With captions the limit is 90 minutes, and without them it's 30.
- Gemini then picks the key concepts, keeping only quotes it can find in the transcript, and everything is saved to the database.

**3. Explain the video**
The person types their explanation in their own words.

**4. Teach the class**
- Maya and Omar each raise a hand with a question ready. Maya only knows what the person told her, while Omar has seen the video.
- The person picks who to answer, for up to 4 questions.
- After each answer, Gemini checks whether it helped. That student then follows up or asks something new.
- "I'll be back: check the video" opens the private explanation, and answering after that saves as `needed_help`.
- The person can skip a question, end the class early, or leave and come back to it.

**5. The class ends**
It shows how many questions they answered, and that's the end of the app today. Scoring, gaps, the assessment and warm-ups aren't built yet (8X-15 onward).

**Before this can run in production:**
- **Deploy the API:** create the Vercel project and add all its environment variables, including `SUPADATA_API_KEY`.
- **Ship the app:** the phone app needs a build pointing at that API, and the browser version would need its own hosting.
- **Fix migrations:** `Deploy migrations` is switched off, so future database changes won't apply on their own.
- **Check speed:** class questions took 10–12 seconds from your Mac because of database round trips to Ireland. Running Vercel in the same region as the database should cut that a lot, though I haven't measured it.
- **Watch Supadata credits:** the free plan covers about 50 new video reads a month. After that, reads should fall back to Gemini, but I haven't seen that happen.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=28 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T09:42:36.989Z
time: 2026-10-31 09:42
chars: 2838

Production now tries Gemini first and falls back to Supadata. On your Mac it still uses `yt-dlp`, then Supadata, then Gemini, because you asked for this order in production only. Here are the two runs in production mode on your Mac:

| Test | What happened | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Gemini working | Gemini read the video, and Supadata wasn't called | 7.5 seconds, 31 segments, 412 words |
| Gemini failing (bad key) | It fell back to Supadata | 9.3 seconds, 30 segments, 412 words |

Supadata also takes over if the YouTube title lookup refuses a video, or if Gemini refuses to transcribe it or finds it too long. If Supadata has no captions either, the person sees the original error.

**The Gemini prompt for reading a video** is sent with the YouTube link attached, at temperature 0, and its reply must be JSON:

```text
Transcribe everything spoken in this video, word for word, from the very start to the very end. Do not summarise or skip anything. Split it into segments of one or two sentences. Give each segment the MM:SS time it starts in the video (H:MM:SS past the first hour). If nobody speaks in the video, return no segments. Never invent speech.
```

**The key concepts prompt** runs next as a system instruction, with the transcript as the message, also at temperature 0:

```text
You read the transcript of an educational video and pick the ideas someone must be able to explain to show they understood it. Pick 5 to 10 key concepts, in the order the video covers them. Use only what the transcript says. Do not add outside knowledge. Skip jokes, asides, greetings, sponsors and requests to subscribe. Give each concept a short title. Give each concept one quote: 8 to 25 consecutive words copied exactly from the transcript, the place where the video states that idea. Never paraphrase, shorten or join separate sentences.
```

**What this order changes:**
- **Long videos cost more:** Gemini now runs before anything knows the video's length. A 2-hour video gets fully transcribed, paid for, and only then rejected as too long. Supadata would then read it if it's under 90 minutes with captions, so you'd pay both.
- **Every new video uses Gemini tokens:** captions were free in tokens, and Gemini is also slower on long videos. Vercel's time limit could stop a long read before it finishes, and that wouldn't trigger the Supadata fallback, because the whole request gets killed.
- **Upside:** Supadata credits are only used when needed, so 100 credits go much further.

A cheap fix for the long-video cost is to get the length first, for example from the YouTube Data API. Tell me if you want that added.

This is uncommitted on the help panel branch alongside that work. The tests pass, but the production build hasn't run yet, and the fallback hasn't been run on Vercel. The run with Supadata used 2 more credits.

Yes, all five AI calls use `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`:

| Call | What it does | Temperature |
|---|---|---|
| `video_transcript` | Reads the video when Gemini is used | 0 |
| `key_concepts` | Picks the 5–10 concepts | 0 |
| `student_turn` | Writes Maya's and Omar's questions | 0.7 |
| `student_follow_up` | Checks whether an answer helped | 0 |
| `class_help` | Writes the private "I'll be back" explanation | 0.2 |

The model name is set separately in four files, with `student_follow_up` sharing the `student_turn` setting. That means you can change one call's model without touching the others.

I'd keep flash-lite for everything except scoring (8X-15) when that's built. It handles the simple calls fine and it's quick, and I've only seen it make small mistakes, like Omar asking two questions in one turn. Scoring decides what someone got right or wrong, and the rest of the app builds on those gaps, so it's the one call where a stronger model could be worth the extra cost. Test both on the same explanations before deciding.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=30 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T10:12:03.351Z
time: 2026-10-31 10:12
chars: 847

Yes, all five Gemini calls use structured output. Each one sets `responseMimeType: 'application/json'` and a `responseJsonSchema`, so Gemini has to reply in the exact shape we define:

| Call | Shape it must return |
|---|---|
| `video_transcript` | Segments with a time and text |
| `key_concepts` | Concepts with a title and quote |
| `student_turn` | `{ question }` |
| `student_follow_up` | `{ follow_up: true or false }` |
| `class_help` | `{ explanation, segment }` |

The replies are also checked on our side, so a malformed reply counts as a real failure and never becomes partial data. Each of the student and help parsers has a test for that, and transcript and key-concept results get their own safeguards. For example, concept quotes must actually appear in the transcript, and the help timestamp comes from the transcript, not the AI.

Here's a prompt to paste into namemyapp's box. It's written for naming the app, so it covers what makes the app different and what to avoid:

```text
A mobile learning app where you learn by teaching. Paste a YouTube video you watched, explain it in your own words, then teach it to two AI students. Maya is a curious beginner who only knows what you tell her. Omar has seen the video and checks you against it. They raise their hands, ask about what you got wrong or left out, and you pick who to answer. If you get stuck, say "I'll be back", learn it privately from the video, and return to answer. Afterwards you see your gaps, and short warm-ups from students who remember you bring them back over time, so what you learned actually sticks.

Audience: students, self-learners and anyone who watches educational videos but forgets them.

Feeling: warm, smart and a little playful, like a classroom that's on your side, not a strict exam app.

Name ideas should suggest teaching, explaining, a mentor and student, or learning that sticks. The working name is Protégé, so suggest alternatives in that spirit.

Rules: short, easy to say and spell, works worldwide, one or two words. Avoid generic words like "Learn", "Study", "Edu" or "AI" on their own. Prefer an available .com, .app or .ai.
```

The gaps and warm-ups in this prompt aren't built yet, but they're in your brief, so a name should cover the full idea.

If the box only takes a short line, use this:

```text
A mobile app where you learn a YouTube video by teaching it to AI students who ask about what you missed, then remember you and bring your gaps back in warm-ups. Warm, smart, playful. Name should suggest teaching or learning that sticks.
```

Pick the name with Arif before buying a domain, because 8X-31 is still unassigned and the choice affects you both.

chars: 1588

My pick is **Chalkback**. It sounds like a real brand, and it holds the app's main idea: you're at the chalkboard, and the class talks back.

**Best fit for the idea:**

| Name | Why it works |
|---|---|
| **Chalkback** | Your students answer back, and it's memorable |
| **Teachback** | "Teach-back" is a real learning method, so the name explains the app, though it sounds more clinical |
| **Handsup** | The raised hands are your signature moment; simple and playful |
| **Showhands** | A twist on "show of hands", with more personality |

**Warm and playful:**

| Name | Why it works |
|---|---|
| **Pupil** | Short and warm, and you're the one with students |
| **Classmate** | Friendly and easy to say, but common |
| **Tinyclass** | Cute, and it fits two small students who remember you |
| **Sensei** | You become the teacher, but it's already widely used |

**About remembering:**

| Name | Why it works |
|---|---|
| **Sticky** | Matches the hackathon theme, "Learning that sticks" |
| **Recallroom** | Clear, but a little long |
| **Stuck** | Bold, since it's where you get stuck and what sticks, but it may sound negative |

I'd compare **Chalkback**, **Handsup** and **Teachback** with Arif. Protégé is a nice word, but it points at the wrong person: in your app, the user is the teacher and the AI students are the protégés. It's also hard to type with the accent.

I haven't checked domains, trademarks or app store names for any of these. Run your favourites through namemyapp first, since common words like Pupil and Sticky are likely taken or already used by other apps.

The Vercel project is set up and connected to GitHub, but it can't deploy yet because it has no environment variables. Copying your secrets into Vercel was blocked by the permission system, and I didn't try to work around that.

**Done:**
- Created project `chalkback` on your Vercel account (`shayankhanx1x`).
- Connected it to `devv-shayan/8x-hackathon`, deploying `main` to production, with previews for branches and PRs.
- Set the root directory to `apps/web` and the framework to Next.js.

**Needs you:** add these in Vercel → `chalkback` → Settings → Environment Variables, for Production and Preview:

| Variable | Value |
|---|---|
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local` |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local` |
| `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local` |
| `GEMINI_API_KEY` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local` |
| `SUPADATA_API_KEY` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local` |
| `DATABASE_URL` | Same as `apps/web/.env.local`, with `&connection_limit=1` added at the end |
| `REVENUECAT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | New, from the command below. **Production builds fail without it** |
| `CRON_SECRET` | New, from the command below |
| `DASHBOARD_API_KEY` | New, from the command below |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` | `https://chalkback.vercel.app`, or the real domain Vercel gives you |
| `PLAY_SLUG` | `chalkback` |

Run this once for each new value:

```bash
openssl rand -hex 32
```

Don't add `DIRECT_URL` or `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEV_COUNTRY`, because the rules say they don't belong on Vercel. Don't paste any of these values in chat.

**After that:** the next merge to `main` deploys automatically, so CD is done with no custom pipeline. Tell me when the variables are in, and I'll check the deployment, read a real video through the live API, and check that class questions are faster there.

**Three things to know:**
- **CI and deploys:** your CI runs on GitHub as before, but Vercel doesn't wait for it, so a merge deploys straight away. That's another reason to merge only on green.
- **Migrations:** `Deploy migrations` is still off, so database changes won't apply on their own. It needs `PRODUCTION_DATABASE_URL`, the `resolve-baseline` run, and the `PRISMA_BASELINE_RESOLVED` variable. I can do that with you as a separate step.
- **GitHub account:** Vercel is under `shayankhanx1x` and the repo under `devv-shayan`, and the connection worked. If Vercel's GitHub app ever loses access to that repo, deploys will stop.

I also undid a line `vercel link` added to `.gitignore`, and left the blocked `.env.local` check alone. That link step said it created a `.env.local` in the repo root, which only holds Vercel's own local settings. It isn't tracked by git, and you can delete it.

All 11 variables are on Vercel for both Production and Preview, and all of them succeeded through the CLI. I generated `REVENUECAT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `CRON_SECRET` and `DASHBOARD_API_KEY` new, and no values were printed. Any value you generated with `openssl` isn't needed now.

- **Nothing has deployed yet:** connecting the repo doesn't build `main` by itself, and Vercel builds on the next push. The repo rules also say a manual production deploy needs your explicit OK for that one deploy.
- **Preview uses your real database:** there's no separate staging database, so anything done on a preview deploy writes to the same hosted Supabase. That's fine for a hackathon, but keep it in mind.
- **Generated secrets:** `DASHBOARD_API_KEY` has to match your stats dashboard's connection, and `REVENUECAT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` has to match RevenueCat if you use it. They're only in Vercel, so if you need them elsewhere, generate new ones and set both sides.
- **Not set:** `PLAY_INGEST_KEY` and `PLATFORM_BASE_URL`, so no platform marketing emails, and PostHog on the web, so no web PostHog analytics. None of these block a deploy.
- **Site URL:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` assumes `chalkback.vercel.app`. Vercel may assign a different address, and I'll correct it if so.

After the first deploy, I'll read a real video through the live API, which is also the first real test of the Gemini-first flow on Vercel.

The first production deploy failed, but the fix is ready in PR #13 and its preview build works. Nothing is live in production yet: both production attempts ended in errors, and the production address returns 404.

**What went wrong:** `apps/web` used `@app/contract` without declaring it. Your local install links every workspace, which hid the problem, but Vercel only installs what's declared. The fix is one line in `apps/web/package.json`, plus the lockfile.

**On the preview build:**
- The build finished as `READY`.
- `GET /api/app/v1/config` returned a real response.
- `POST /api/app/v1/videos` without a login returned 401, as it should.

Merging deploys production automatically. GitHub's typecheck/test/build and migrations checks are still running, so it shouldn't merge until they pass.

**Things to know:**
- **Duplicate attempts:** my retry started a second production deployment, because the first request had gone through. Both failed the same way, so no harm done.
- **Preview login:** preview deploys sit behind Vercel's login, so public phone or browser testing needs the production address.
- **Production address:** `chalkback.vercel.app` isn't assigned yet, and Vercel's alias list doesn't include it. If it isn't assigned on the first successful deploy, I'll claim it or correct `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`.
- **Node version:** the project setting says Node 26.x, though the rules say the `engines` range in `apps/web/package.json` decides. I'll confirm it runs on Node 22 from the production build.

Once production is up, I still need a signed-in user to test a real video read on the live API. I'll do that through the app, or with a temporary test user that I delete afterward.

The whole app works on production, and it's dramatically faster. I ran the full flow on [chalkback-app.vercel.app](https://chalkback-app.vercel.app) with your account: a new video, a full 4-question class, the help panel and a follow-up.

| Step | Local Mac (hosted DB) | Production |
|---|---|---|
| Next question after an answer | 10–12 seconds | **0.7–2.1 seconds** |
| Starting a class (2 questions) | About 10 seconds | **2.1 seconds** |
| Help panel | 3.6 seconds or more | **1.6 seconds** |
| Reading a new 15-minute video | Not measured | 25.4 seconds |

Moving to Dublin worked: the slowness was the distance to the database.

**Confirmed working on production:**
- **Gemini-first reading:** the logs show Gemini read the video, and Supadata wasn't called, so no credits were used. It found 7 key concepts with real quotes.
- **Maya and Omar:** both raised hands and asked questions. Maya stayed within the explanation.
- **Help panel:** showed a real 7:20 timestamp, and that turn saved as `needed_help`.
- **Follow-up, finally seen on screen:** after my vague answer, Omar said "That still does not explain how getting fired actually caused those new companies to happen."
- **Reaching question 4:** the class ended with "Class finished. You answered 4 questions."

**Problems found:**
1. **Repeated questions:** Omar's question was written at the start, before I answered Maya, so it came out nearly the same as hers: "How can getting fired actually lead to starting successful companies…". That's a side effect of keeping hands up, and it's the most noticeable flaw left in the class.
2. **CORS error:** `/api/lifecycle/session` is blocked for the web app, because I only allowed `/api/app/v1`. It didn't stop anything in this run, but that call won't work from the web app until it's allowed. The fix is a small addition.
3. **Maya's wording:** she invented "when nobody wants to buy what you are making", which isn't something you taught. It's a mild example, and a real student might wonder the same thing.

I marked 8X-36 Done, because Supadata is live and the first deploy is checked. The comment notes that the fallback hasn't yet fired in the live app, and that the video length check is still separate work. There's no record of it in Linear as its own issue yet. This test added another class on your hosted account, and the session's work is on `main`.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=56 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T13:17:20.675Z

Both fixes work, and every local check passes, including the production build. They're uncommitted on `devvshayan/class-rethink-questions`.

**1. Lifecycle error**
The app can now reach `/api/lifecycle/session` from the browser. This turned out to matter more than the error suggested, because that call records signups, last-seen time and region, which your welcome and win-back emails and per-market numbers rely on. Locally the call now returns 200, but the production rule only takes effect once this deploys.

**2. Repeated questions**
- **At the start:** Maya writes her question first, and Omar is told to avoid it. Maya is never shown Omar's waiting question, so his video details can't leak to her that way.
- **Omar's job:** he now asks about an important idea from the video that you got wrong or left out. In the real class he asked about remembering death, which my explanation never mentioned, instead of copying Maya.
- **After each answer:** a yes/no check asks whether your answer already covered the waiting question. If not, the question stays word for word. If so, the student writes a new one. That check got 4 out of 4 test cases right, and in the real class it replaced Omar's question after I covered it.

My first two attempts failed. Omar copied Maya word for word, then a "keep it or change it" prompt changed the wrong questions. Both are gone.

**Costs and limits:**
- **Slower start:** the students now write one after another, so starting a class takes about a second longer, around 3 seconds on production by my estimate.
- **Extra AI calls:** each answer now adds the yes/no check, and a covered question adds another call. I estimate 3–5 seconds on production in that case, up from 0.7–2.1.
- **Remaining overlap:** Maya can't see Omar's waiting question, so she could still ask something close to it.
- **Questions can change:** if you peek at a waiting question and then answer the other student, it may change. A student rethinking is realistic, so I kept it.
- **Replacement wording:** Omar's new question opened with a reaction, "That explanation about stripping away fear…", and added "market demand", which the video may not cover.

**Test class left open:** clicking "End class" did nothing, because the API was still restarting after the build. That class is saved on your hosted account for the Steve Jobs video, so opening that video will resume it instead of showing the explanation step. Just press "End class" there. The local API is running again in [Web API 3001](#terminal/c13).

I'd do all three of my picks, the first option under each. They're small changes to the same class code, so they fit in the PR that's waiting.

### 1. Maya overlapping Omar's waiting question
- **A. Widen the yes/no check (my pick):** it already asks "did the teacher cover this?". It would also ask "does this overlap what another student just asked?". If it does, Omar writes a new question. This adds no AI call, and Maya still never sees his question.
- **B. Have Omar always write after Maya:** this is simple and needs no check, but Omar would change questions far more often, for no reason.
- **C. Leave it:** two students sometimes ask similar things in real classes. It's the cheapest option, but it's the flaw you noticed.

### 2. A question changing after you've read it
- **A. Show that it changed (my pick):** a small line like "Omar changed his question" appears when it does. It fits the classroom feel, because he's reacting to what you said. It needs a small screen change and no database change.
- **B. Lock a question once you've picked it:** this is the most predictable, but it needs a new column and a new request, and a locked question can go stale, which is what we just fixed.
- **C. Leave it silent:** there's no work, but it can look like a glitch, especially on camera.

### 3. Omar's new question reacting and adding outside ideas
- **A. Tighten the prompt (my pick):** add two rules: replacement questions don't comment on your answer, and Omar only asks about what the video actually says. This takes minutes, but it reduces the problem without guaranteeing it's gone.
- **B. Make Omar quote the video:** he'd give a short quote with each question, and the server would check that it's in the transcript, as key concepts already do. This is the strongest fix, since it enforces "the video is the source of truth", but it adds work and possible retries.
- **C. Show the source:** add "from 7:20" on Omar's questions. It would look great and prove he's using the video, but it depends on B.

I'd do B and C after scoring lands, since a question tied to a timestamp fits gaps and the assessment screen well. For now, Option A gets you most of the way.

Want me to make the three picks now and add them to the waiting PR?

All three are done, and the changed-question behaviour works in a real class. This time the test class ended cleanly, with no question left open.

**The real class run:**
- Omar's waiting question was about how dropping out led to the calligraphy class.
- I answered Maya in a way that also covered that.
- Omar's question then became: "Oh, that answers what I had in mind. How does looking in the mirror every morning asking if today were your last day actually help you make big life choices?"
- That new question comes from the video, and Maya followed up correctly because my answer dodged hers.

**What changed:**
1. **Overlap:** the check now returns three results: open, answered, or overlap. On overlap, Omar quietly writes a new question, and he doesn't claim you answered it. Maya never sees his question.
2. **Changed questions:** when your answer covered a student's question, they say so in their own voice and move on. There's no separate notice on screen.
3. **Staying with the video:** Omar now only asks about what the video says. In 3 test questions and the real class, he stuck to it.

**How accurate it is:**
- **The check:** it got 5 of 6 test cases right. The miss was two calligraphy questions asking slightly different things, which is a borderline call. It's a first version on 6 test cases, so expect the odd mistake.
- **Same opening line:** Omar copied my example wording, "Oh, that answers what I had in mind.", each time. That's fine once in a class, but it would sound repetitive if it came up twice.
- **Maya can still overlap in one direction:** she can't see Omar's question, so overlap only gets fixed when Omar is the one still waiting.

**Gemini limits:** during testing I hit a 429 from Gemini, "exceeded your current quota". It cleared within minutes, so it looks like a per-minute limit on your plan, not a daily one. Each answer can now use up to three AI calls, so a fast class is more likely to hit it. If it happens right after an answer, the rethink step is skipped harmlessly, but a failure in the follow-up or new question can still cause trouble. Run a test class right before you record the demo.

All checks and the production build pass. Everything is uncommitted on `devvshayan/class-rethink-questions`, along with the lifecycle and back-button fixes, and none of it is live until it's merged. Production speed with these extra calls still needs measuring after that. The local API is running in [Web API 3001](#terminal/c14).

   - Product now named **Chalkback**: paste YouTube video → explain → teach AI students Maya and Omar → gaps → warm-ups. Maham's feedback: functionality first, Loom under 1 min per feature (camera on, speaking), out-of-box initiative.
   - This stretch: fix Maya leaking video details; build raised hands/End class matching Arif's wireframe; fix listed issues 1–6; Supadata for cloud captions; Gemini first in production then Supadata; help panel and its gaps; name and rebrand to Chalkback ("chalkback it is , update everything"); CI/CD deploy to Vercel; move to Dublin; host the app itself; fix lifecycle CORS and repeated questions; back-button bug; overlap and changed-question behaviour; latest "Merge when green" for PR #15.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Monorepo: `apps/mobile` (Expo SDK 55, expo-router, web via export), `apps/web` (Next.js 15, API `/api/app/v1`, Prisma 6), `packages/contract` (Zod, `endpoints.ts`; every write endpoint needs a `body`, path params lowercase), `packages/brand`. npm workspaces, Node 22 (`PATH=$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v22.23.4/bin:$PATH`; shell default 24, `vercel` CLI lives on the 24 path).
   - All AI: `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, structured output (JSON schema + Zod). Calls: `video_transcript`, `key_concepts`, `student_turn`, `student_follow_up`, `student_waiting_check`, `class_help`. Gemini per-minute 429 seen in testing.
   - Video read order: production (`NODE_ENV==='production'`): oEmbed title + Gemini transcript, then Supadata fallback on any error; otherwise yt-dlp → Supadata (only if yt-dlp null or rate-limited) → Gemini. Supadata: `/v1/transcript` (lang en, mode native, chunkSize 100) then `/v1/metadata`, sequential with one 429 retry after 1.2s; 2 credits per read, free 100/mo. | S5 Explain | Explain/Teach/Gaps tabs, Type or Speak | Typing only, no tabs | **App behind**; Speak depends on your decision in 8X-29 |
| S8 Gaps | Score, missed points, timestamps, what students said | One line: "You answered 2 questions" | **App behind**; needs scoring (8X-15) |
| S9 Warm-up, S10 Topic | Full screens | Not built | **App behind** |

**Missing from the wireframes:**
- The student saying "that answers what I had in mind".
- A student changing their question while waiting.
- Resuming a class.
- The onboarding screens.
- The tab bar. Explore and Activity are still template screens, and they belong in the cleanup (8X-30).

**The one real conflict:** the wireframe says "The source stays hidden", but Home shows every key concept with its timestamp and a quote from the video before the person explains anything. That hands them the answers, which undermines the point of "Learning that sticks".

I recommend hiding the concepts until after the class, since it's a small change and they'd fit naturally on the Gaps screen later.

The wireframes need only a small update, so Arif should keep them as the target and just change the names, the sign-in options and the skip button. The other screens are still the plan, and I'd leave them as they are. The older `wireframes-screenshots/mobile` folder should be labelled as outdated or removed. I also didn't check the 30- and 90-minute video limits from S3; the length check is still on the backlog.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=67 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]

Scoring is the step right after the class ends, but it isn't built yet. In today's app, the class stops at "You answered 2 questions." This is the plan from [brief.md](docs/product/brief.md), and it's Arif's part (8X-15).

**What happens next**
1. The class ends after 4 questions or when you press End class.
2. The AI scores your explanation, and you get an Assessment screen.
3. Every concept you missed or got wrong is saved as a gap.
4. Each gap comes back later as a one-minute warm-up from a student who remembers you.

**What it's based on**

Your explanation is compared with the key concepts found in that video, like the 7 we saw for the Steve Jobs speech. The AI gets your explanation, the key concepts and the transcript, then marks each concept:

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Correct** | You explained it properly |
| **Missed** | You left it out |
| **Wrong** | You said something incorrect |

That gives the headline score, like "6 of 8". The brief doesn't define a pass mark, and the score isn't a grade on how well you write.

Questions are tracked separately, as answered, needed help or skipped. The app already saves these. Students also add a short in-character reaction, but the brief doesn't say this changes the score.

The score stays hidden until the class ends, so you explain from memory instead of chasing points.

**Why it matters**

The score isn't the goal; it's how the app finds out what didn't stick. Without it, nothing connects a class to the next one:
- **It shows your gaps:** you see exactly what you missed, with a timestamp to rewatch that moment in the video.
- **It drives the warm-ups:** a gap is due after 2 days. If you get it right, it's due again 5 days later, and a second right answer closes it. If you get it wrong, it's due again in 2 days.
- **It delivers the theme:** those reviews around day 2–3 and day 7 are what make this "Learning that sticks".

So without scoring, there are no gaps, warm-ups or topic page. That's why it's the main blocker, and Arif should say today whether it's on track, since the brief wants the whole loop ready for Feedback 2 on Monday.

I've read all three calls. You didn't ask for anything specific, so here's what changes what I told you earlier.

**Answered: scoring has an owner and a date.** Arif said he'll do the scoring part and the explanation screen tonight (Oct 31). So the question I raised about whether scoring is on track is settled, for now at least. If it lands, the first version of the learning loop is complete, as you both said on the call.

**Things to tell Arif before he starts tonight:**
- **Explanation screen:** one already exists inside [class/[topicId].tsx](apps/mobile/app/class/[topicId].tsx), so he should pull `main` and build on it, not start a new one.
- **Where scoring plugs in:** when a class ends, the app shows a one-line finish message. Each question's outcome is already saved, so his Assessment screen can start there.
- **The explanation is saved too:** the scoring call also needs the key concepts and the transcript. The transcript and concepts are already what the students use.
- **Gaps are separate:** scoring must save missed and wrong concepts as gaps, or warm-ups have nothing to work from. He only mentioned scoring, so check this is included.
- **Key concepts on Home:** he should know about the conflict I flagged, where the app shows the answers before the person explains. If you hide them, the Assessment screen is their natural new place.

**Things from the calls worth correcting or watching:**
- **Wireframes:** yesterday Arif said they weren't final and needed your review, and they were marked done in Linear anyway. That probably explains why they're out of date, so review them with him when he updates the names.

Good news on the phone. Was that the live link in the phone's browser, or the native app? 8X-27 means the native app or an emulator, so I've left it open.

Nearly everything to clean up is template content that isn't about Chalkback yet.

**Remove from the app**
- **Explore tab and the detail screen:** these show the template's demo items, and `listItems` is their only use. Removing them also removes the `items` API.
- **Activity tab:** it shows template notifications, and nothing in Chalkback creates them.
- **Tabs after that:** only Home and Profile remain. Later, Topics could take the empty slot.

**Remove from the API**
- `items` and probably `tasks`, `activity` and `notifications`, along with their tests. The app has no direct calls to the last three, but I'd confirm that nothing else uses them before deleting.

**Rewrite for Chalkback**
- **Marketing site:** the title still says "your one-line pitch goes here", and the landing page shows demo phone screens with demo data. It needs a real pitch and real Chalkback screens.
- **Support and Privacy:** the Privacy text says "This template application", and the Support answers talk about demo data and API adapters. Both are public, so they need real text.
- **Account deletion text:** it should say what's actually deleted: videos, classes and answers.

**Hide, don't delete**
- **Premium and pricing:** the Profile card, the upgrade screen and the pricing page describe a paid plan we don't have. I'd hide the entry points and leave the payment code, since payments are one of the template's load-bearing parts.
- **Admin panel and Play Stats API:** leave these alone. The stats API is a non-negotiable, and the admin panel works.

**Add**
- **Loading screen:** replace the blank first load on the web.
- **Icon and splash:** that's 8X-31, not this issue, and it needs artwork. A native icon change also means a store build.

**Decisions I've made**
- **Onboarding:** keep it. It only asks for a name and appearance, so it's harmless.
- **Other screens:** I'd leave the sign-in screen and Settings as they are.
- **Events:** I'll remove the event tracking for deleted screens, and `check:events` will catch any mismatch.

I'd do this in one PR. It shouldn't touch `class/[topicId].tsx`, so it won't clash with Arif's scoring work tonight. One overlap: if his Assessment screen adds a new tab or a route in the tab layout, whoever merges second will have a small conflict.

Yes, some of it, so I'd change my plan: hide more and delete less. It's a hackathon with six days left, and what matters is that judges don't see template content, not that the code is tidy.

| Part | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Activity tab, `activity` and `notifications` APIs** | Hide | Warm-up reminders ("Maya has a question for you") could use these |
| **Explore tab** | Hide | Its list and search are a good starting point for a Topics screen |
| **Premium, pricing and upgrade** | Hide | We'd need them if Chalkback ever charges |
| **Detail screen, `tasks` API** | Hide | Not needed now, and hiding costs nothing |
| **Demo data in the app and on the marketing site** | Replace | This is what people actually see as template content |
| **`items` API** | Keep for now | It goes unused once Explore is hidden, and removing it can wait until Topics decides whether to reuse it |

So nothing gets deleted in this cleanup, except demo content that's replaced. Anything deleted later is still in git and can be brought back.

The work gets smaller too: hide the tabs and the Premium entry points, then rewrite the marketing page, Support, Privacy and the account deletion text, and add the loading screen. The downside is unused code staying in the repo, and code quality is rated, so I'd delete whatever's still unused before the final submission on Nov 5–6.

I'll go with this version if you say to start.

**Likely cause (not confirmed):** his own code comment says "Gemini TLS from this machine is flaky". Scoring now runs before the class starts, and a dropped connection isn't turned into a proper message, so it shows "Something went wrong on our side". His API terminal log will show the real error at the moment he presses Start the class.

**A real bug this exposes:** if scoring fails for any reason, the whole class fails to start, and this can happen on production too. The class should start anyway and score afterwards or retry. A busy Gemini is the likely trigger, and that's the risk you'd face in a demo.

**Scoring may be too generous:** my test explanation never mentioned dropping out, but "Dropping Out" was marked correct. That's only one result, so test a few more explanations under 8X-19.

**His Discord points**
- **Live link:** send him https://chalkback-app.vercel.app. Your Vercel previews need a login, but this production link doesn't.
- **Email code failing:** I can't tell why from here. If he was on the live or hosted setup, Supabase's built-in email has a low hourly limit, which is a common cause and easy to hit while testing. He should try again on the live link and send you the exact error if it fails.
- **YouTube errors:** "sometimes" fits Gemini being busy or his connection, but he should send the video link and error message.
- **Other transcribers:** Qwen, Granite, Cohere and Whisper need audio, and that's the download route you already rejected as too slow. They'd also need hosting, so I'd skip them.

One of your two tickets is built but not yet committed, and the other is blocked until you run one command.

**8X-38, migration: not done.** I confirmed it's missing from production, but the permission check blocked applying it, so I stopped there. It only makes a warm-up's answer and right-or-wrong result optional. Run this yourself from the repo root:

```bash
cd apps/web && node --env-file=.env.local ../../node_modules/.bin/prisma migrate deploy
```

Your shell needs Node 22 for this. Your `.env.local` currently points at the hosted database, which is the target here.

**8X-37, class start: built, not committed.**
- If scoring fails, the class now starts anyway, and the students ask about every concept instead of only the missed ones.
- The class is scored again when it ends, so the Assessment still gets its score.
- If that second try also fails, the Assessment says the score isn't available, which Arif's screen already handles.

All 11 local gates pass, including tests and the build. I added one test, covering the case where a class has no score.

**Not tested:** I tried a simulated Gemini failure, but the browser couldn't reach the local server, so that request never ran. I removed the temporary failure code afterwards. The failure path itself hasn't been exercised, so that checkbox on 8X-37 stays open and the ticket isn't marked done.

**Before this merges:** tell Arif, because it changes `class-session.ts` and `db/classes.ts`, and his gap work (8X-17) will likely touch the same files.

The scoring failure is now simulated, and the fix holds up. I added [class-session.test.mts](apps/web/src/server/classroom/class-session.test.mts), which runs the real class code with the scoring call to Gemini failing with a network error. The database is in-memory for this test, and the other Gemini calls are faked.

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| Scoring works at class start | Students focus on what was missed or wrong |
| Scoring fails at class start | The class still starts, with 2 students asking about every concept |
| You end that class and scoring now works | Scored at the end, with results returned |
| You answer the last question | Also scored at that point |
| Scoring fails both times | The class still ends, with no score and no error |
| Class already has a score | Not scored a second time, so no extra Gemini call |

All 6 tests pass. To prove they catch the bug, I put the old behaviour back for a moment, and 4 of the 6 failed. Then I restored the fix. Typecheck, lint, format and the full test suite pass with it in place; I didn't rerun the other gates after adding this test file.

**Still not covered:** this wasn't a run in the deployed app with Gemini actually down. The test doesn't check how the app screen shows the "score isn't available" message.

This isn't a bug in the app or in PR #20: your Mac's network address changed. The local app is still calling your API at `192.168.1.11:3001`, but your Mac is now `192.168.1.12`, so those requests never arrive. The browser shows `ERR_ADDRESS_UNREACHABLE`, while the API itself is running fine on the Mac.

That old address was set for testing on your phone, and your Wi-Fi router gave the Mac a new one. It also explains the grey loading blocks I saw on Profile earlier. "Admin · My Team" in your screenshot confirms this is the local development build, not the live app.

**To fix it**, update the address in `apps/mobile/.env.local`. I'm not allowed to read or change that file, so run this yourself from the repo root:

```bash
sed -i '' 's/192\.168\.1\.11/192.168.1.12/g' apps/mobile/.env.local
```

Expo only picks up the change on a clean restart. Stop it in the [Expo QR](#terminal/c3) tab, then run this:

```bash
cd apps/mobile && npx expo start --clear
```

Your router can change the address again, so if this comes back, compare it with:

```bash
ipconfig getifaddr en0
```

To avoid this while testing on the same Mac, point the file at `localhost` instead. Your phone still needs the network address, though. The live app at [chalkback-app.vercel.app](https://chalkback-app.vercel.app) doesn't use this setting, so it isn't affected.

Maya is fixed: she no longer gets the video, so she can't repeat details you never taught her. Before, one AI call decided who spoke and wrote the question, so it had to see the transcript even on Maya's turns. Now the code picks the speaker first, and only Omar's turns include the transcript and key concepts.

**Real test:** I ran 12 AI turns using a made-up video full of details the explanation never mentioned, such as thylakoid, Rubisco, night breathing and cave plants.
- **Maya:** she spoke 7 times, and everything she said came from the explanation. For example: "how can thin air turn into something solid like a plant?"
- **Omar:** he still used the video, asking for "the exact part of the leaf where the water gets split".
- **Follow-ups:** vague answers still got a follow-up from whoever asked. The one case I tested for a skipped question moved on in the unit tests, not in a live call.

**Other changes:**
- Every question was opening with "Wait,", so I banned that opener. The next run stopped doing it.
- Your answers now get a separate quick AI check to decide on a follow-up, so most questions take two Gemini calls instead of one.

**Things to know:**
- **Rate limit:** my testing hit a Gemini 429 once, and the extra call makes that more likely in a fast class. I didn't check what the app shows you when it happens.
- **Repeated questions:** Maya asks much the same thing again after Omar's turn, because her question isn't really tied to what just happened. All 3 of her test questions that hit this were about air turning into something solid.
- **Not tested:** I ran this directly against Gemini, not through the app screen, so the class flow in the UI still needs a run with this change. The test explanation and transcript were also short.

The tests, typecheck, lint and the other local checks pass. The production build still has to run before the PR.

Raised hands and "End class" both work in the app now, matching Arif's wireframe. I tested them in the browser with your signed-in session, which also covered the app-screen check the Maya fix was missing.

**How the class works now:**
- **Hands up:** both students raise a hand, and the screen shows "Hands up: pick who to answer" and "Question 1 of 4".
- **Picking:** tapping a student shows their question, and the other student's question stays hidden until you pick them.
- **After an answer:** the other student keeps their hand up, and only the student you answered thinks of a new question, so it costs no extra AI questions overall. Their first questions are the exception: both are written at the start, so that's two calls.
- **End class:** it finishes straight away and drops any questions you didn't pick, and I tested this with and without answering first.
- **Maya:** a question nobody has picked yet isn't shown to the other student, so Omar's video details can't reach her that way. She'll only hear them if you answer him.
- **Events:** I added `class_hand_picked`, and `class_finished` now records whether you ended early.

**One rule removed:** I dropped the limit of one follow-up in a row. Follow-ups used to be limited so a student couldn't keep pressing you, but now you can pick someone else or end the class.

**Problems still there:**
- **Reaction before any answer:** Maya's first question can open with "That got pretty mixed up in my head", as if she's reacting to something when you haven't answered yet.
- **Slow questions:** each one still takes 10–12 seconds, even though the AI part is 1–2 seconds. My earlier guess about the database is still untested, but the slowness is confirmed on this version.
- **AI failure after an answer:** if Gemini fails at that point, your answer is saved but that student gets no new hand. With no other hand up, the class would look finished, or stay stuck on your answer until you restart. This isn't handled yet.

**Not tested:**
- A full class to question 4; I stopped after one answer.
- The follow-up question itself. The vague-answer check ran, but my tap on Omar's second question missed, so I never saw what he asked.

**Test data:** my two test classes on the photosynthesis video are saved in your account, and Maya and Omar may bring them up in later classes as past conversations.

Yes, a few more. Most of these come from reading the code, and I only saw the second one happen in the app. They're in the order I'd fix them.

**Bugs**
1. **Unheard questions can leak into later classes:** if you press back with hands still up, those questions stay saved. The next class treats them as past conversation, marked "(no answer)". That means Omar's video details can reach Maya, and the students can mention questions you never heard. "End class" clears them, but leaving the screen doesn't.
2. **Leaving loses the class:** the screen doesn't resume, so pressing back or reloading sends you to a blank explanation. The old class stays unfinished.
3. **Memory isn't tied to the video:** students get your last 6 questions from any earlier class, so a question about photosynthesis could turn up in a class on a different video. That can feel like real memory or like a mistake.
4. **Answers can appear out of order:** the conversation is sorted by when each question was created, not when you answered it. If you answer Omar first and then an older Maya question, Maya's shows above his. The AI reads the class in that same wrong order.

**Things that feel wrong**

5. **False reactions:** "That got pretty mixed up in my head" and "That explanation actually makes sense" appear on opening questions. The second is praise, which your rules ban, and neither is a reaction to an answer.
6. **Undeserved praise:** "Class finished. Nice teaching." shows even if you end the class without answering anything.
7. **Very short explanations work:** typing "hi" starts a full class.
8. **Omar isn't aimed at what you missed:** he picks from all the video's concepts, because scoring (8X-15) doesn't exist yet. Asking about something you left out is partly luck for now.

**Gaps a judge will notice**

9. **The class is a dead end:** it finishes with a sentence and no gaps, score or next step.
10. **You can't get back to a video:** Home has no list of past videos, so you have to paste the link again.
11. **It still looks like the template:** the title says "MyApp", and the Explore and Activity tabs are still there.
12. **The student cards are bare:** Arif's wireframe shows "Beginner" or "Critical" on each card, and ours show only the name.

I'd fix 1, 4, 5, 6 and 7 before opening this PR, because they're small and sit right inside this change. Then 2 and 3 can follow as the next piece of work, while 8 and 9 wait on scoring.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=109 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:28:42.790Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:28
chars: 1049

Because on your Mac it's a program that's already installed, and Vercel only ships your app's code. The API calls `yt-dlp`, or `uvx yt-dlp`, as an outside command. Vercel runs each request in a small temporary Node.js environment, which normally has neither of those. `yt-dlp` isn't an npm package either, so `npm install` won't bring it along.

That part is fixable: we could include the standalone `yt-dlp` program in the deploy so the code calls it directly.

The bigger risk is YouTube itself. It often blocks or challenges requests from cloud servers like Vercel's, even when your home connection works fine. So even with `yt-dlp` included, caption requests could fail, and we already saw 429 rate limits while testing from your own machine. When that happens, the app falls back to Gemini, which is slower and limited to 30-minute videos.

I haven't tried any of this on Vercel, so this is what I expect, not something I've seen fail. Testing it takes one deployed video read, and that's the first thing I'd do before relying on a public link.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=110 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:31:20.531Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:31
chars: 2259

Captions through `yt-dlp` won't work on Vercel, and both of my concerns were right. I ran a throwaway function on Vercel's servers (US East) three times on your photosynthesis video, and all three failed the same way:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `yt-dlp` already there | No |
| `uvx` or Python already there | No |
| Downloading the standalone `yt-dlp` | Worked, and it ran |
| Reading the video on YouTube | **Blocked**: "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" |

So including `yt-dlp` in the deploy wouldn't help, because YouTube refuses Vercel's servers.

**What this means for a hosted app:**
- **Video reading should still work, through Gemini only:** the code already falls back when `yt-dlp` fails, and Gemini fetches the video itself, so YouTube's block on Vercel shouldn't matter. I haven't tested that on Vercel.
- **Reading will be slower:** captions take seconds, while Gemini took 14 seconds for a short video.
- **Long videos have no length check:** without `yt-dlp`, the app doesn't know how long a video is, so the 30-minute limit never applies. A long video would go to Gemini anyway, costing more and possibly hitting Vercel's time limit.
- **Some public videos may fail:** the fallback gets the title from a YouTube lookup that also refuses videos with embedding turned off, so those would show "We can't open this video." That lookup also comes from Vercel's servers, so I can't promise YouTube allows it.

**Ways around it:**
1. **Use Gemini only when hosted:** the simplest option, and enough for a hackathon. Add a way to get the video's length, such as the YouTube Data API, so the limit still works.
2. **Pass YouTube cookies to `yt-dlp`:** this is what the error suggests, but it ties the app to a personal Google account, can get that account flagged, and breaks when the cookies expire. I wouldn't do it.
3. **Run reading on a small separate server or proxy:** the most reliable for captions, but it's more to build and pay for.

I'd go with option 1 if you want a public link. For Monday's feedback, nothing changes, because your local setup still uses captions.

The throwaway project is deleted from your Vercel account and no longer appears in your project list. It held no secrets, and none of your repo's code was deployed.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=111 session=1f02878a-1847-414e-b657-d91660105c2b]
timestamp: 2026-10-31T07:33:19.051Z
time: 2026-10-31 07:33
chars: 4069

Yes, there are real options besides Gemini, and I'd use a hosted transcript API, starting with Supadata. The block is on the server's IP address, not on `yt-dlp` itself, so switching libraries usually doesn't help. The fix is to get the captions through an address YouTube doesn't block.

| Option | What it is | Cost | Catch |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hosted transcript API**, like [Supadata](https://supadata.ai/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api) or [TranscriptAPI](https://transcriptapi.com/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api-compared) | Your server makes one normal HTTPS call, and they deal with YouTube | Supadata lists about 100 free credits a month; TranscriptAPI reportedly starts around $5 per 1,000 requests | Depends on another company; prices vary between sources |
| **[youtube-transcript-api](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api) with rotating residential proxies** | Open-source Python library; its maintainer recommends Webshare's residential proxies | Library is free, proxies are paid | Python, so it doesn't fit our Node API directly; [one user was still blocked on AWS](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api/issues/593), and free-tier proxies don't work |
| **`yt-dlp` with [bgutil PO tokens](https://pypi.org/project/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider/)** | Plugin that makes requests look more legitimate | Free, plus a server to run it | Its own docs say datacenter addresses usually still need cookies, so this is the risky account-tied route again |
| **Fetch from a home or office machine** | [Run only the transcript step there](https://dev.to/jeffreyinman/fixing-sign-in-to-confirm-youre-not-a-bot-for-youtube-transcripts-on-a-server-3i1b) and send results to the cloud | Free | A machine has to stay on; useless for a real product |
| **YouTube's official Data API** | The official route | Free quota | Reportedly needs OAuth to download captions, usually from the video's owner, so it likely won't work for any public video. I didn't verify this |

**Why Supadata:**
- **Smallest change:** it's one HTTP call added before the Gemini fallback, with no proxies or Python.
- **No-caption videos:** comparison articles say it has an AI fallback for these. I'd keep our Gemini fallback anyway.
- **Free tier:** at about one credit per transcript, it should be enough for the hackathon demo.
- **Video length:** our limit check needs the duration, so I'd have to confirm the API returns it.

**Before relying on these:**
- Most of the comparison pages come from the vendors themselves, so treat their rankings, speed and reliability claims with caution, and check prices on their own pricing pages.
- These services and proxies work around YouTube's blocks, which is a grey area under YouTube's terms. That's fine for a demo, but worth rechecking if this becomes a real product.

If you go ahead, you'd need to sign up for a free key and add it to the env file yourself, without pasting it in chat. I'd then run the same test from Vercel, because a vendor's claim isn't proof it works there.

Sources:
- [Guide to cloud IP blocks](https://github.com/hxckya/youtube-transcript-ip-blocked-guide)
- [What fixes RequestBlocked in 2026](https://dev.to/lmslay/youtube-transcript-api-requestblocked-on-your-server-what-actually-works-in-2026-1pak)
- [Fixing the "not a bot" error on a server](https://dev.to/jeffreyinman/fixing-sign-in-to-confirm-youre-not-a-bot-for-youtube-transcripts-on-a-server-3i1b)
- [Vercel and Lambda blocks issue](https://github.com/hallelx2/youtube-transcript-ts/issues/1)
- [youtube-transcript-api](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api)
- [Webshare still blocked report](https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api/issues/593)
- [Supadata comparison](https://supadata.ai/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api)
- [TranscriptAPI comparison](https://transcriptapi.com/blog/best-youtube-transcript-api-compared)
- [bgutil PO token provider](https://pypi.org/project/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider/)
- [Data API suggestion in TwoRiversReporter](https://github.com/AndreRobitaille/TwoRiversReporter/issues/87)

Supadata is built, but I can't test it against the real service until you add an API key. It's uncommitted on a new branch, `devvshayan/supadata-captions`, cut from the latest `main`, which already includes your merge of PR #10. The tests pass with my two new ones, but they only cover turning Supadata's captions into our transcript format, not a live call.

**How a video gets read now:**
1. **`yt-dlp` goes first:** it's free, and your local setup should keep working the same way.
2. **Supadata is next:** it only runs if `yt-dlp` is missing, blocked or rate-limited, which on Vercel should be every time. It fetches the title, the length and any existing captions.
3. **Gemini is the fallback:** it handles videos with no captions.

With a Supadata key, hosted reads get the video's length back, so the length limits should work again.

**To add your key:**
1. Sign up at [supadata.ai](https://supadata.ai) and create a key.
2. Add this line to `apps/web/.env.local`, and don't paste the key in chat:

```bash
SUPADATA_API_KEY=your_key_here
```

3. Add the same variable in Vercel when you deploy.

Once it's in, I'll restart the API and read a video with `yt-dlp` hidden, so it has to go through Supadata. I'll also check how many credits a read costs, because the docs I read didn't say.
